## Phase 10.8 — ALLOW NEGATIVE STOCK: the HEAD-oracle harness (branch `claude/confident-ellis-dedef5`, 2026-07-27) — **HARNESS ONLY; NO production code written yet**

**Log-continuity note (2026-07-27):** this file's per-slice log runs through **Phase 10.5 (schema v46)** above. The interim work that carried the schema **v46 → v49** (the UI-defect campaign PRs and the voucher-numbering / service-invoicing features) was **not appended here** — the commit chain on `main` is the record for it. **⚠️ R5 was also missed for most of THIS session** (the entry below is a back-fill written at the point the gap was noticed, not as-we-went). Per-slice logging resumes here.

- **Session start — baseline established BEFORE any change.** Branch `claude/confident-ellis-dedef5` fast-forwarded onto **`9c2bded`** (a **pure FF** — `c655dc2` is an ancestor, nothing rebased or rewritten). **Gate re-run by the orchestrator PERSONALLY (not taken from an agent's word):** build **0W/0E**; **3491 tests green — Ledger 1261 · Io 359 · Sqlite 184 · Desktop 1687**. Schema **`Schema.CurrentVersion = 49`** (`src/Apex.Ledger.Sqlite/Schema.cs:124`).
- **Commits so far on this branch — 3, NONE pushed, NO PR:**
  - `b2f6c40` chore(oracle): .gitignore for harness scratch
  - `a6fd61b` docs(plan): Phase 10.8 — allow-negative-stock
  - `2b84ba2` docs(plan): reverse the `AverageCost` deferral
- **THE DEFECT — read from SOURCE, not from memory.** `StockValuationService.Consume` (`src/Apex.Ledger/Services/StockValuationService.cs:368-384`) loops `while (remaining > 0m && layers.Count > 0)` and **silently DISCARDS** whatever it cannot draw, then clamps `runningQty`/`runningCost` to 0; `LayerValue` (:255-265) **ignores real on-hand entirely** (`_ = closingQty;`). **Worked example:** buy **10 @ ₹10** → oversell **15** → buy **20 @ ₹12** leaves layers of **20 @ ₹12 = ₹240** while `InventoryLedger.OnHand` = **15** ⇒ an implied **₹16/unit — a rate that never existed**. FIFO-correct is **₹180**. That is **+₹60 overstated onto Balance-Sheet Stock-in-Hand and P&L**.
- **THE HEAD-ORACLE HARNESS — built BEFORE any production change (`tools/HeadOracle/`, uncommitted so far).** Two processes, two private engine copies: a **pristine `.oracle-baseline/`** materialised by `git archive 9c2bded` (manifest + path-list + commit-pin **verified every run**), and a **live arm re-copied from `src/` every run** so it can never be stale. Both arms diffed as **integer paisa**. It went through **six build/audit rounds** and **five adversarial audits**; verdicts in order: **NOT-READY → NOT-READY → TRUSTWORTHY-WITH-GAPS → NOT-READY → TRUSTWORTHY-WITH-GAPS**. **This — building the oracle first and auditing it adversarially before touching `src/` — is the whole reason the failures below were found in a harness instead of shipped in a fix.**
  - **Audit #1 — three CRITICALs, each PROVED by running a MUTATED engine through the real harness** (not argued on paper): (1) an ordinary **`ApexLedgerProject` env var redirected BOTH arms to a foreign engine** while the report still printed **pristine hashes and `CLEAN`**; (2) a violation **whose key already existed at HEAD was discarded REGARDLESS OF MAGNITUDE** — a mutation manufacturing **₹100,000 of phantom stock** came back `CLEAN`; (3) an engine that **THREW on every negative-stock valuation** was certified **`CLEAN` and credited with 16 RESOLVED violations**.
  - **Audit #2's CRITICAL — the most instructive failure of the session.** Checks **3 and 8 were MUTUALLY UNSATISFIABLE** on subject **`G6-001`**. On a book where **25 units were issued but only 10 were ever bought**, **NO** closing value can put implied COGS/unit inside the band of rates actually paid, and the check's magnitude **INCREASES with closing value** — so moving that subject from HEAD's **₹0 (a wiped asset — the actual defect)** to the correct **₹78.16** scored as a **REGRESSION**. **The harness would have steered the fix into the EXACT failure this project already shipped once** (a positive-quantity floor turning a diagnosable **−₹120** into a plausible **₹0**). **Fixed** by classifying structurally-unsatisfiable subjects into their own bucket, **excluded from the introduced/worsened verdict in BOTH directions** and printed in full. **⇒ RULE: an oracle whose ACCEPT state has never been OBSERVED is not known to have one.**
- **🔴 A PROJECT-MEMORY CLAIM WAS REFUTED — AND IT WAS A TAUTOLOGY.** The recorded claim that *"HEAD's moving average is in band on every conservation trace — it was never the broken one"* is **FALSE**. It came from a reference implementation deliberately written to **ECHO HEAD's own averaging rule**, so `AverageCost` **could never be shown wrong by it**. Two **independently written** implementations — a C# reference in the harness and an out-of-band **Python model validated BYTE-FOR-BYTE against real HEAD output on 95/95 subjects** — agree: on `In 5 @₹1000.07 → Out 25 → In 20 @₹1000.07 → In 30 @₹0.37`, HEAD closes at **₹12,007.50** where debt-aware is **₹11.10** — **₹11,996.40 of phantom asset (~1082×)** on an item with **₹25,012.85 ever spent** — and **no band / spend / COGS check catches it** (implied **₹400.25** sits inside `[0.37, 1000.07]`). **6 of 184 `AverageCost` subjects disagree.** The **sign FLIPS on lot order**: cheap-then-dear **understates by 12.92%**, dear-then-cheap **overstates**. `G6-001` is **8 units physically counted on the shelf, reported at ₹0.00**. **Honest qualifier:** per family, `AverageCost`'s COGS violations are never *worse* than FIFO's — but **"no worse than the method we know is broken" is not "in band."** **⇒ TRANSFERABLE LESSON: an oracle that REPRODUCES the code under test proves NOTHING. Calibrate a reference where the engine is TRUSTED, then use it where it is NOT; label any echo as carrying no correctness evidence.**
- **▶ USER DECISIONS — 2026-07-27 (R12). RECORDED EXPLICITLY because the harness relies on them and NOTHING IN THE REPO EVIDENCED THEM until this entry** (an audit correctly flagged one of them as *"a claim about a human ruling that nothing in the repository evidences"*).
  1. **Fix `AverageCost` too, oracle-gated — the deferral is REVERSED** (`plan.md` amended, `2b84ba2`). **Consequence:** the harness check that **byte-locked `AverageCost` to HEAD** therefore **FORBADE the fix** and has been **INVERTED** — `AverageCost` is now a **first-class point-oracle subject** and **HEAD is convicted on all 6 subjects**.
  2. **Keep auditing the harness until an INDEPENDENT adversary returns TRUSTWORTHY**, verifying **both** that a correct engine is **ACCEPTED** and that several **distinct wrong engines are REJECTED**.
  3. **Shortfall valuation policy** — when a debt is settled by a movement carrying **NO purchase rate** (a physical count with a debt outstanding, or an unrated inward), the units are valued through the engine's **existing best-available-cost chain** (`CostContext.NoRateInwardCost`: **running average → strictly-positive `StandardCost` → last rated inward → 0**). Chosen because it is **the rule HEAD already applies to any unrated inward**, so it introduces **no new policy**. **NINE of the harness's 32 golden constants are valid ONLY under this ruling — including the `G6-001` crux (8 × ₹9.77 = ₹78.16).**
- **WHERE THE HARNESS STANDS.** A three-method **reference-conformant engine reaches ACCEPTED at FULL coverage** (**306/306 declared cells, 25,677 rows, nothing shrunk**); **six DISTINCT wrong engines are REJECTED** — re-rating a debt at the old rate · a **positive-quantity floor clamping to ₹0** · a correct closing value with a **wrong `IssueValue`** · an **`AverageCost`-only defect** (**the very engine the old byte-locked check certified**) · a defect confined to the **structurally-unsatisfiable** subjects · a **debt-repayment off-by-one**. It **CONVICTS HEAD** on **70 closing values, 143 issue values, 68 company totals and the 6 `AverageCost` subjects**. The reference's **debt branch** — which **cannot be calibrated against HEAD, because HEAD has no correct debt behaviour** — is pinned by **32 hand-derived golden constants**, each written up as checkable arithmetic and **independently RE-DERIVED by the auditor, who agreed with all 32**.
- **THE CANDIDATE S-A PATCH (derived in a sandbox — NOT applied to `src/`).** **+38 / −8 lines** in `BuildLayers`/`Consume`, **of which 20 are comment**. A **debt carries NO rate** (nothing has been bought to cost it) and is **NEVER re-rated**; an inward **repays it FIRST, at that lot's own rate**, and only the **surplus** becomes a layer; a **physical count writes the debt off**. `runningQty`/`runningCost` semantics are **unchanged**, so the **no-rate fallback chain is untouched**.
- **▶ OPEN AT THE TIME OF WRITING.** **Round 6** is anchoring the reference's **issue-value arm** with hand-derived goldens — **audit #5** showed it was the **last verdict-issuing arm with no external anchor** and **moved 68 of 120 demands (40× on the crux) while the harness still printed SOUND**. **NO production code has been written: `src/` and `tests/` are BYTE-IDENTICAL to `9c2bded`** (`StockValuationService.cs` SHA-256 = `befae5c2d9aec89205ba7e0b294529660da025e719e65c5c20a957716a539395`); gate **still 3491 green — Ledger 1261 · Io 359 · Sqlite 184 · Desktop 1687**.
- **⚠️ LESSONS FROM THIS SESSION (transferable — reinforce them):** (1) **an oracle that reproduces the code under test proves nothing** — calibrate where the engine is trusted, use it where it is not, and **label any echo as evidence-free**; (2) **an oracle whose ACCEPT state has never been OBSERVED is not known to have one** — always prove a *correct* engine passes, not only that wrong ones fail; (3) **mutation-prove the harness itself** — every one of audit #1's three CRITICALs surfaced only by running a deliberately-broken engine through the real pipeline; (4) **a recorded project-memory claim can be a tautology** — check *how* a past conclusion was reached before relying on it.

## Phase 10.8 — ALLOW NEGATIVE STOCK: **STOPPED AND BANKED** (branch `claude/confident-ellis-dedef5`, 2026-07-29) — **USER DECISION (R12): revert the engine, bank the harness and the findings**

**Continues — and CLOSES — the 2026-07-27 section above.** That section's header ("**HARNESS ONLY; NO production code written yet**") is **SUPERSEDED**: production code *was* written this session, **five separate times**, and **every attempt has been reverted**. **THE HONEST HEADLINE: the valuation prerequisite is STILL UNSOLVED. What changed is that it is now PRECISELY CHARACTERISED** — and that characterisation, plus an audited oracle that can convict a wrong fix, is what this session delivers. **EIGHT attempts in total: three in earlier sessions** (see the `negative-stock-valuation-open-problem` memory note) **and five here. EVERY ONE OF THE EIGHT PASSED THE FULL TEST SUITE. FOUR also PASSED THE ORACLE and were then CONVICTED BY ADVERSARIAL REVIEW.** Not one was caught by the suite alone — which is the entire justification for the harness.

- **🔴 THE STRUCTURAL FINDING — the reason we stopped, and the thing that makes this session different from the three before it: ANY PREDICATE-GATED SCOPE CREATES A VALUATION CLIFF AT ITS BOUNDARY.** The last candidate scoped the debt rule to **single-key items** (one (item, godown, batch) key). That scoping is **correct where it applies** and **provably inert where it is not** — a **20,736-row engine-vs-engine sweep**, with the key count computed **OUTSIDE the harness** so the sweep could not be graded by its own predicate, shows **0 of 15,552 two-key rows move**, and the sweep is **discriminating, not vacuous**: forcing the predicate true moves **3,712**. **But the predicate is not a property of the business — it is a property of the BOOK, and an ordinary posting changes it.** **ONE ORDINARY INTERNAL GODOWN TRANSFER — which moves nothing into or out of the company, changes no quantity the company owns, and costs nothing — flips an item's WHOLE HISTORY between the two models:**
  - **₹40,000,001.20 with the transfer vs ₹25,000,000.75 without** — and the gap is **UNBOUNDED in the lot rate**, so no magnitude ceiling exists.
  - It **SURVIVES A SAME-DAY ROUND TRIP**: send to Store B and bring it straight back, Store B ends **empty**, and the item stays on the phantom model **for ever** — the cliff is a one-way ratchet, not a transient.
  - It puts **₹15,000,000.45 between closing stock on 20 Apr and 25 Apr WITH NO ECONOMIC EVENT** between the two dates.
  - **HEAD is CONTINUOUS here (`jump=0.00`).** The discontinuity is **CREATED by the change**, not exposed by it. **⇒ A ninth attempt that scopes by any predicate — key count, method, item flag, date — will reproduce this. Scoping is not the fix; it is the failure mode.**
- **▶ THE DESIGN THE REAL FIX NEEDS — the session's main FORWARD-LOOKING output; record this as THE PREREQUISITE (plan `NS-8`).** Two requirements, and **both are load-bearing**:
  1. **Value stock on the SAME (item, godown, batch) key as QUANTITY.** Today valuation is **item-level** while quantity is **per-key** — that desync is itself one of the eight defects below.
  2. **Make a Stock-Journal TRANSFER CARRY ITS COST LAYERS between keys**, instead of re-deriving them at the destination.
  - **Re-keying WITHOUT cost-flowing transfers was TRIED and it BROKE ORDINARY TRANSFERS: ₹5,000,002.37 of Stock-in-Hand on ₹1,000,003.73 EVER SPENT — where HEAD was EXACTLY RIGHT.** Requirement 1 without requirement 2 is a **regression**, not a partial fix. **Do not attempt them separately.**
- **▶ THE EIGHT MEASURED FAILURE MODES** — one line each; **the full reproducing books are in `tools/HeadOracle/README.md`, which is the HANDOVER DOCUMENT — read it before touching stock valuation.** In order found:
  1. **Forward-looking cost chain** — a **physical count priced by a LATER purchase**: **₹9,000,000.27** on **9 units** against **₹1,001,001.33** ever spent.
  2. **The same forward look on an UNRATED INWARD** — **₹259,999.74** on **₹11,001.29** ever spent.
  3. **Valuation CHANGED on a NEVER-NEGATIVE book** that merely drains to **exactly zero** — breaking the **byte-identity contract** and moving **ER-13 export bytes**.
  4. **The MULTI-GODOWN WIPE** — **Stock-in-Hand ₹0.00** on a **guard-legal book where nothing is ever negative**; honest value **₹1,159.50**; **unbounded**.
  5. **The item-level / per-key DESYNC made 13.5× WORSE** — the FIFO recovery case went **₹240.00 → ₹3,243.90** on **10 units**.
  6. **Per-key replay BROKE AN ORDINARY TRANSFER** — **₹5,000,002.37** on **₹1,000,003.73** ever spent (the case that forces requirement 2 above).
  7. **A count in a godown that NEVER HELD STOCK** booked **every unit at ₹0.00**.
  8. **The per-EVENT debt gate DISAGREED with the product's per-DATE definition of negative stock** — one **same-day dip in an UNRELATED THIRD GODOWN** took **₹237.30 → ₹79.10**.
- **▶ WHAT IS BANKED, AND WHY IT MATTERS.** The **committed HEAD-oracle harness** (`275c395`, extended further this session): **two processes, two private engine copies, integer-paisa diffs, 198 hand-derived GOLDEN CONSTANTS (133 closing + 65 issue) independently re-derived by TWO reviewers**, and **engine-vs-engine byte-identity**. It went through **six build rounds** and an adversarial-audit chain that ran **NOT-READY → NOT-READY → TRUSTWORTHY-WITH-GAPS → NOT-READY → TRUSTWORTHY-WITH-GAPS → TRUSTWORTHY**, and **it CAUGHT EVERY ONE OF THE EIGHT DEFECTS BEFORE ANYTHING SHIPPED — which is exactly what the three prior sessions lacked.** It now reports **REJECTED against HEAD**, **correctly**, because **HEAD has the defect**. **Its verdict is SCOPED to single-key subjects**, where its reference is validated; **multi-key subjects are printed as INFORMATIONAL, NOT A VERDICT**, because the **item-level reference is NOT a valid oracle for transfers** — a scoping that is itself a finding, and one the harness states about itself rather than hiding.
- **▶ TWO PRE-EXISTING DEFECTS FOUND AND DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED** (found while investigating; **both are in HEAD today**, neither is caused by this work, and fixing either inside a reverted change would have been scope creep):
  1. **`StockValuationService.MovementEvents` SKIPS `Allocations` / `DestinationAllocations` on a Physical-Stock-typed voucher** (the `continue;` at **`src/Apex.Ledger/Services/StockValuationService.cs:180`**, block **:175-181**) **while `InventoryLedger.ApplyToKey` (`src/Apex.Ledger/Services/InventoryLedger.cs:193-207`) applies them for EVERY voucher type** — so quantity and value read the same voucher differently.
  2. **The item-level / per-key VALUATION DESYNC itself** (valuation item-level, quantity per-key) — the thing `NS-8` requirement 1 exists to fix.
- **▶ USER DECISIONS — 2026-07-27 / 2026-07-29 (R12). Recorded WITH DATES because the harness relies on them and nothing else in the repo evidences them:**
  1. **(2026-07-27) Fix `AverageCost` too, ORACLE-GATED** — reversing an earlier deferral whose premise was a **tautology** (detail in the section above).
  2. **(2026-07-27) Keep auditing the harness until an INDEPENDENT adversary returns TRUSTWORTHY.** **Now SATISFIED** — the chain closed at **TRUSTWORTHY**.
  3. **(2026-07-27) Shortfall valuation uses the engine's EXISTING best-available-cost chain** (`CostContext.NoRateInwardCost`) — no new policy.
  4. **(2026-07-27) Fix the desync PER-KEY and report what moved.** **⚠️ LATER REVERSED ON EVIDENCE (2026-07-29)** — per-key **broke ordinary transfers** (failure mode 6). **The reversal was correct, and so was the original decision at the time it was made**: per-key *is* required; what the ruling could not know was that it is **inert without cost-flowing transfers**.
  5. **(2026-07-29) STOP AND BANK rather than attempt a NINTH scoping.** The decision that closes this phase's current line of work.
- **⚠️ WORKFLOW TRAP — THIS COST REAL TIME, RECORD IT: three workflow phases were KILLED MID-RUN by session limits, and EACH ONE LEFT FILE EDITS ON DISK** — **twice including PRODUCTION CODE written by an agent that NEVER REPORTED.** A dead workflow does **not** mean a clean tree. **ALWAYS `git status` AND HASH THE TREE before trusting a result or resuming a phase.** (Compounds the existing `workflow-liveness-before-relaunch` lesson: liveness is not the only thing a killed phase lies about — **so is the working tree**.)
- **▶ STATE AT CLOSE (verify, don't quote — R5).** Branch **`claude/confident-ellis-dedef5`**, HEAD **`96a9c74`** before the close-out commits, **17+ commits ahead of `origin/main`, NOTHING PUSHED, NO PR.** **`src/` and `tests/` are BYTE-IDENTICAL to `9c2bded`.** Suite back to the pre-session baseline **3491 green — Ledger 1261 · Io 359 · Sqlite 184 · Desktop 1687**; build **0W/0E**. Schema **v49** (negative stock never reached its **v50**).
- **⚠️ LESSONS FROM THIS SESSION (transferable — these are the reusable output):** (1) **a predicate-gated scope is not a safe subset — it is a DISCONTINUITY**, and an ordinary posting can walk a book across it; prove **continuity across the boundary**, not just correctness on each side; (2) **"passes the full suite" carries almost NO information about a valuation change** — eight for eight; (3) **"passes the oracle" is not enough either** — four of the eight did, and adversarial review convicted them; the oracle bounds the *search*, review still has to bound the *design*; (4) **an inert-by-measurement change can still be WRONG** — inertness was proven on 15,552 rows and the defect lived entirely in what happens when the book *stops* being inert; (5) **a killed workflow leaves edits on disk** — hash the tree; (6) **stopping with a precise characterisation is a RESULT**, and is worth more than a ninth attempt that repeats the cycle.

## Phase 10.9 — TALLY-GAP REMEDIATION: five parallel streams BUILT, REVIEWED and MERGED (branch `claude/confident-ellis-dedef5`, 2026-08-03) — **schema v49 UNCHANGED · 3651 green (Ledger 1281 · Io 361 · Sqlite 210 · Desktop 1799) · NOT PUSHED, NO PR**

**Twelve commits above `bc95728`** — five feature streams, their five merges, the cross-stream interaction tests and the Tally audit. **Every gate below was re-run by the orchestrator PERSONALLY, not taken from an agent's word.** This entry is the R5 record for the session and the R6 amendment that put `plan.md` back in agreement with the repository: **before this, the plan listed backup/restore BOTH as an excluded Phase-10 module AND as the mitigation for its own top-ranked data-loss risk R-7 — while the feature was already built and committed**, and it had **no entry whatsoever** for any of the five merged features. That is the governing document disagreeing with the code, which is exactly what R6 exists to prevent. `plan.md` now carries **Phase 10.9** (GAP-1…GAP-6), a **carve-out STATUS banner on Phase 10**, a rewritten **R-7** mitigation cell, a corrected **status header** and an amended change log.

- **▶ WHY THIS WORK EXISTED — the audit came first (`6124a25`, three documents, 1,852 lines, all committed).** `docs/tally-version-and-voucher-gap-audit.md` (release timeline 4.5 → Prime 7.1 with **every source labelled primary or secondary** — the official pre-2009 release notes are no longer served and archive.org was unreachable, so most 4.5–9 dating is **secondary and marked as such**; what Tally 9 added over 7.2; and the features later versions **removed or renamed** that would mislead anyone cloning from 7.2). `docs/voucher-entry-specification.md` (TallyPrime's **four-layer field gating** — F11 capability → F12-on-master visibility → the master's own value → F12-on-voucher visibility, a field appearing **only when all four permit it**, with the corpus warning about the layer-1/layer-2 trap on **four separate pages**). `docs/tally-gap-decisions.md` (**24 decisions, D1…D24**, each with mutually-exclusive options and a recommendation-with-reason). **Researched from the ten licensed TallyPrime PDFs and official documentation; the user's installed Tally 7.2 was NOT opened, listed or launched.**
- **▶ THE FIVE STREAMS — what each one actually fixed. Read the defect, not the feature name; in every case the app looked fine and the books did not.**
  1. **GAP-2 — cost allocation as PARALLEL SETS, not a partition** (`claude/gap-2` → **`aed9a50`**). The validator **summed allocations across ALL cost categories** and demanded the total equal the line, which **REJECTED THE CORPUS'S OWN WORKED EXAMPLE** — ₹5,000 travel booked to **Branch → Kolkata AND Department → Marketing** simultaneously (**Study Guide pp.101-102**). **Multi-category cost accounting was impossible.** Now enforced **per category**, with **legacy-book rehydration** so vouchers saved under the old rule still load, plus corrected cost-report labelling.
  2. **GAP-4 — voucher-type reachability** (`claude/gap-4` → **`7bfc2c6`**). **Credit Note and Debit Note had NO MENU ROW ANYWHERE** — those strings **did not occur in `MainWindowViewModel.cs` at all** — and were reachable only by shortcut. **Physical Stock advertised F10**, which **official TallyPrime help assigns to "view list of all vouchers"**; its real key is **Ctrl+F7**, which was **bound to nothing** — so the type had **no keyboard route while the UI claimed one**. Voucher-type selection fell back to **`?? FirstOrDefault(BaseType == x)`**, which **silently opened DEACTIVATED types** and made a **second Sales series unreachable**; now resolved **by identity**, with a clear message when no active type exists. **Bill settlement was a sixth unconverted call site** still posting under a deactivated type.
  3. **GAP-3 — backup and restore** (`claude/gap-3` → **`e90a169`**). **Did not exist.** Version-stamped against schema **v49** and **refuses a restore the running build cannot handle**; destination guard so a backup cannot silently overwrite a live company database; connection disposed on a failed store construction (it previously **locked the file for the process lifetime**); **restore round-trip test** included.
  4. **GAP-1 — voucher-entry core** (`claude/gap-1` → **`828fc9f`**). **Both invoice Accept paths built the party leg WITH NO BILL ALLOCATIONS**, and `Outstandings` counts **only lines that have them** — so a company invoicing **normally** had an **EMPTY Receivables report, EMPTY ageing and NOTHING TO SETTLE, silently, with no error.** Adds **Single Entry mode** on Contra / Payment / Receipt (**Receipt/Contra Account = Dr, Payment Account = Cr**) and enables **Purchase Accounting Invoice**, whose **TDS/RCM detection now reads the Particulars lines** it was previously blind to.
  5. **GAP-5 — real batch allocation on the item-invoice screens** (`claude/gap-5` → **`374b221`**). Batch allocation was a **FREE-TEXT STRING** on the **two screens where a pharma/FMCG user actually buys and sells batched goods** — no picker, no available balance, no expiry capture, no split, no reconciliation. **The proper sub-screen already existed, wired to the wrong screens**; it now serves item-invoice line entry, **gated so an item that does not maintain batches behaves exactly as before**. **Stock valuation deliberately untouched and byte-identical.**
  6. **GAP-6 — cross-stream interaction tests** (**`688ccd2`**). **12 tests, odd paisa throughout**, covering what **no single stream could test, because until the merge those combinations did not exist**: a batch-split line and a two-reference bill split reconciling to the same total **and the refusal when they differ by one paisa** (the seam where GAP-5's billed-quantity-driven invoice total feeds GAP-1's exact-equality Accept gate); short-billing moving the bill-wise target onto the **billed** basis; bill-wise on a **TDS-carved purchase accounting invoice** splitting across two references; an invoice under a **second, non-predefined Sales type** carrying both bill-wise and batch behaviour, and a **deactivated type being neither listed nor openable**; a voucher carrying **both** a parallel cost set **and** a bill split; and a **backup round-trip carrying all of it**.
- **▶ WHAT ADVERSARIAL REVIEW CAUGHT PRE-COMMIT — the justification for the per-stream review gate (R10). Three of these are money defects on vouchers that BALANCE.**
  - **GAP-1 — CRITICAL.** A **reverse-charge purchase credited the supplier ₹15,777.78 of tax it never charged** and **claimed input GST TWICE**, on a voucher that **balanced perfectly, so nothing caught it.**
  - **GAP-3.** A restore failing **at the swap** had **already deleted the target's `-wal` / `-shm` / `-journal`**, leaving the **live company unrecoverable**. Fixed: the original is **never degraded until the replacement is verified in place**.
  - **GAP-5 — two money defects.** Splitting a line across batches **CHANGED THE POSTED INVOICE TOTAL** and the party-ledger amount; and short-billing computed the live total **and the GST/TCS base** on the **Actual** rather than the **Billed** quantity.
- **▶ THE GATE — four exact PER-PROJECT predictions in a row, each merge gated before the next. Recorded as all four counts, NOT as a total** (see the rule at the end of this bullet):

  | after | commit | Ledger | Io | Sqlite | Desktop | total |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | base | `bc95728` | 1261 | 359 | 184 | 1687 | **3491** |
  | GAP-2 + GAP-4 | `014f3ca` | 1281 | 361 | 188 | 1718 | **3548** |
  | GAP-3 + GAP-1 | `6580da9` | 1281 | 361 | 210 | 1767 | **3619** |
  | GAP-5 | `9688235` | 1281 | 361 | 210 | 1787 | **3639** |
  | interaction tests | `688ccd2` | 1281 | 361 | 210 | 1799 | **3651** |

  **Build 0W/0E throughout**; the final row **confirmed identical across three separate runs** — one `--blame-crash`, two solution-level. Each stream was built in an **isolated worktree cut from `bc95728`**, individually gated, adversarially reviewed, fixed, then **merged ONE AT A TIME with a full gate after each merge** — the reason every count above is attributable. **⚠️ WHY THE FOUR NUMBERS AND NOT THE TOTAL — the standing rule on this project, now written into `plan.md` §6.2 so it survives: A GREEN TOTAL CARRYING THE WRONG PER-PROJECT COUNTS IS A CONTAMINATED RUN, NOT A PASS.** That is how **a truncated Desktop run once reported "Passed! 610" against a real 1635 and looked IDENTICAL to success** — the total is the one field that cannot detect it. **A future session cannot apply the rule against a bare total. The four numbers ARE the check**, so every suite size written into `plan.md` or `memory.md` carries all four.
- **▶ THE ONE CONFLICT — hand-resolved and gated before commit; record the mechanism, not just the fact.** `VoucherEntryViewModel.cs`: GAP-1's **227-line bill-wise block** and GAP-5's **75-line batch block** both inserted **immediately after the `InventoryLines` declaration** — an **add/add at one point**. **A PLAIN CONCATENATION WOULD HAVE BEEN MALFORMED:** each side ended **mid-method**, and the single brace below the conflict could only close **whichever side landed last**, leaving **`InvoiceBillAllocationsOk` unterminated with every batch member parsed as a local inside it**. Resolution kept **both** plus **one brace at the seam**; whole-file balance verified **584/584**.
- **▶ USER DECISIONS — R12. Recorded WITH DATES because nothing else in the repo evidences them.**
  1. **(2026-08-01) TallyPrime is the FIDELITY TARGET; Tally 7.2 is a CHECKLIST ONLY** (`docs/tally-gap-decisions.md` **D1**). The user evaluates against a **2005 product, ~20 years and five product generations behind**, and **all ten `tally/` PDFs are TallyPrime documents — there is NO 7.2, Tally 9 or ERP 9 primary material in the corpus at all.** 7.2 feedback is **triaged through the audit's known divergences before anything is logged as a defect**: things the user "knows" Tally does — **Ctrl+V / Alt+I as separate mode keys, Credit Note on Ctrl+F8, the 1990s menu tree** — are **7.2 behaviours TallyPrime deliberately REMOVED**, and Apex is **correct not to have them**. **The installed 7.2 copy is out of bounds and was never opened.**
  2. **(2026-08-01) OPTIMISE FOR COMPLETENESS OF VOUCHER ENTRY.** The prioritisation rule: voucher entry is where the audit found the defects that **cost money**, so breadth-of-entry beats polish elsewhere. **This is why GAP-1 and GAP-5 were scheduled ahead of report and master work.**
  3. **(2026-08-02) Goods-return STOCK PARITY on Credit / Debit Note is APPROVED — BEHIND AN ORACLE — and is ⚠️ NOT YET BUILT** (**D3**). **`ItemInvoiceStock.Counts()` still ends with `type.BaseType is VoucherBaseType.Purchase or VoucherBaseType.Sales`**, so a **Credit Note cannot carry inventory lines at all**: a sales return **credits the customer but leaves the goods off the books**, a purchase return **debits the supplier but leaves phantom goods on hand**, and **the drift COMPOUNDS with every return**. **GAP-1 deliberately EXCLUDED it** for exactly this reason (its commit message says so). It touches the posting validator, `Counts()`, inventory replay ordering, stock valuation and the existing GST CN/DN linkage — **Robert & Bright must stay byte-identical**.
  4. **(2026-08-02) BACKUP/RESTORE is CARVED OUT of Phase 10 and built now; the REST of Phase 10 stays EXCLUDED** (**D12 = A**, **D13 = leave excluded**). **DONE — GAP-3.** TallyVault, Security Control / roles, Edit Log / Tally Audit, split-by-FY, group company and repair/rewrite **remain excluded and unscheduled**.
- **⚠️ LESSONS FROM THIS SESSION — these cost real time; reinforce them:**
  1. **`isolation: 'worktree'` CUTS FROM `main`, NOT FROM THE CURRENT BRANCH.** The **first wave of all five streams was built against `c655dc2`** — **20 commits and 157 files stale, schema v46 vs v49, and the suite 3321 vs 3491 — Ledger 1239 vs 1261 · Io 349 vs 359 · Sqlite 173 vs 184 · Desktop 1560 vs 1687, i.e. WRONG IN ALL FOUR PROJECTS**. **Four streams completed work against it.** **One detected it and refused, proving it FOUR ways**: the schema constant, the per-project test deltas, a cited line **past end-of-file**, and a grep showing **the code its brief described did not exist**. **⇒ ALWAYS VERIFY THE BASE BEFORE BUILDING: `grep CurrentVersion Schema.cs` must match.** (The five streams that shipped are all parented on **`bc95728`** — check the parents, not the branch name.)
  2. **MATCHING TEST COUNTS DO NOT PROVE A TREE IS UNMUTATED.** A **dead reviewer left a mutation inside a PRODUCTION file** — `VoucherEntryViewModel.cs:256`, a **±₹0.50 tolerance replacing the exact-sum bill-wise rule**. A re-gate showed **IDENTICAL COUNTS**, because **no test covered that line**. **⇒ counts prove the COVERED surface is unchanged and say NOTHING about the uncovered surface.**
  3. **FOUR AGENTS DIED MID-RUN THIS SESSION AND EVERY ONE LEFT FILES ON DISK** — three left stray probes, one left the mutation above, one left its **finished deliverable**. **⇒ the tree check after a death is the ONLY thing that reveals what actually happened.** (Compounds the 2026-07-29 workflow trap and `workflow-liveness-before-relaunch`: **liveness lies, and so does the working tree**.)
  4. **AN UNEXPLAINED TEST-HOST CRASH — RECORDED, NOT RESOLVED.** The first full-suite run after `CrossStreamInteractionTests.cs` **crashed the Desktop host** (`Xunit.Sdk.TestPipelineException`, **exit -1**, after **340 of 1799** tests) and **has NOT reproduced in three subsequent clean runs**. **The two obvious explanations were both checked and DO NOT hold:** assembly parallelisation is **already disabled** in `AssemblyInfo.cs`, and **each test project runs in its own process**, so a process-global `ClearAllPools()` **cannot cross assemblies**. **The likelier mechanism:** the test **deliberately overwrites a live SQLite database with 40 KB of `X`** and then asserts a read throws — but **a corrupted-page read may ABORT THE PROCESS rather than raise a managed exception**, which would depend on what SQLite touches first and **would explain the flakiness**. **⇒ EXPECT RECURRENCE IN CI.**
- **▶ STATE AT CLOSE (verify, don't quote — R5).** Branch **`claude/confident-ellis-dedef5`**, HEAD **`6124a25`**, working tree clean. **NOT PUSHED, NO PR, NO UPSTREAM — A12 has not run (R4).** Suite **3651 green — Ledger 1281 · Io 361 · Sqlite 210 · Desktop 1799** (three separate runs agreeing); build **0W/0E**; schema **v49 UNCHANGED** (Phase 10.9 is schema-clean end to end; negative stock still owns the unclaimed **v50**).
- **▶ WHAT REMAINS — in priority order:**
  1. **The R9 REAL-APP RUN — outstanding, and it is the WIDE one.** It now has to cover the **nine newly-merged features PLUS the previous session's voucher numbering and service invoicing — NONE of which has EVER been seen working outside a test harness.** This is the gate item standing between Phase 10.9 and a user go/no-go.
  2. **Goods-return stock parity** (decision 3 above) — approved, oracle-gated, **not started**.
  3. **The negative-stock valuation prerequisite `NS-8` remains UNSOLVED and PRECISELY SPECIFIED** — per-`(item, godown, batch)`-key valuation **AND** cost-flowing stock-journal transfers, **built TOGETHER** (re-keying alone **broke ordinary transfers**). See the 2026-07-29 entry and **`tools/HeadOracle/README.md`, the handover document**. **Nothing in this phase touched stock valuation.**
  4. **The remaining decisions in `docs/tally-gap-decisions.md` are UNANSWERED** — **D2, D6, D8, D10, D11, D14–D24**. They are the backlog this phase drew from; **nothing is built from them without an R6 `plan.md` amendment first.**

## Phases 10.9 (governance tail) · 10.8 (S-B) · 10.10 · 10.11 · 10.12 Wave 0 — THE WRONG-FIGURES CAMPAIGN: 32 previously-unrecorded commits inside a 34-commit range (branches `claude/confident-ellis-dedef5` → `claude/apex-wrong-figures-bc45f4`, 2026-08-04 → 2026-08-14) — **schema v49 → v50 · BUILT and REVIEWED WITH ONE NAMED EXCEPTION · NOT PUSHED, NO PR, NO UPSTREAM, NOT ON ANY REMOTE · `origin/main` still `c655dc2`**

**32 previously-unrecorded commits, inside a 34-commit range above `6124a25`.** The range's first two are the PRECEDING phase's own governance commits and are **already recorded in this file**: `bb8df50` created `plan.md`'s Phase 10.9 section, corrected the R-7 backup/restore contradiction and wrote the §6.2 four-count rule; `6888738` is the "## Phase 10.9" entry directly above this one, whose STATE AT CLOSE names `6124a25`. **⇒ a session measuring "what is unlogged" should run `git log -1 -- memory.md` (→ `6888738`), NOT diff from `6124a25`.** **THE BRANCH MOVED MID-RANGE AND NOTHING ELSE IN THE REPO SAYS SO:** `claude/confident-ellis-dedef5` — the branch the previous entry closes on — **stopped at `18bf524`**, the 8th commit of the range (verified: `git branch --contains 3d5ac44` does not list it, `--contains 18bf524` does); every commit from `3d5ac44` onward lives only on **`claude/apex-wrong-figures-bc45f4`**, which is HEAD. **THE HONEST HEADLINE: almost nothing in this range is a feature. It is 32 commits of WRONG FIGURES** — money printed that the general ledger never posted, three statutory payloads filed with field names and units we invented ourselves, reports that silently omitted the rows that mattered most, defaults that made the operator work around Tally rather than with it, and four rules each built twice and already answering differently depending on which copy a caller reached. **Every one of them passed a green suite before it was found**, and **every one was found by reading a primary source or by an adversarial review lens — not one was found by a test.** **⚠️ "REVIEWED" HAS ONE EXPLICIT EXCEPTION: `b12b8cb` shipped UNREVIEWED and labels itself so in its own body** ("🔴 THIS SLICE IS UNREVIEWED. Read this before trusting any of it." · "There is NO red-proof, NO restoration proof and NO adversarial review of this code"); `ef8f24a` is its review follow-up. **`f2abdbb` was likewise recovered from an engineer that died mid-slice and was reviewed only afterwards.** Nothing is pushed; A12 has not run (R4).

NEVER CITE A BARE D-NUMBER; every use below names its register.**
- **▶ THE RANGE — 34 commits (29 non-merge + 5 merges), both stream merges verified CLEAN.** Linear except for the merges. **⚠️ AN EARLIER CUT OF THIS CHAIN NAMED ONLY 31 OF THE 34 — it dropped `468a96e`, `9041b25` and `2efd433`, which made `c4caeed`'s first parent read as `3d5ac44` when it is `2efd433`. - **🔴 SCHEMA v49 → v50 — ONE COLUMN, AND IT IS THE ONLY COMPANY FLAG IN THE SCHEMA THAT DEFAULTS TRUE.** `Schema.CurrentVersion = 50` at **`src/Apex.Persistence.Sqlite/Schema.cs:146`**, moved by **`a12e651`** and by nothing else — **`git log --all -S "CurrentVersion = 50"` returns exactly that one commit, and THAT is the check that proves it.** v50 = `warn_on_negative_stock INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1` on `companies`; migration `MigrateV49ToV50` at `Schema.cs:3736-3737`, `CreateV1` parity at `:306`, added-column list at `:3743`, downgrade helper in `SchemaDowngrade.cs`. **Everywhere else in this schema "column absent" and "flag off" coincide, so a missed read path is harmless; here they do not** — a read path treating absence as `false` silently switches warnings OFF on every upgraded book. Documented at `Schema.cs:108` and `:118`; `Company.WarnOnNegativeStock = true` at `src/Apex.Ledger/Domain/Company.cs:268`. **⚠️ DO NOT RE-DERIVE THIS FROM COMMIT BODIES — the earlier draft of this entry claimed "every one of the ten commits after `a12e651` is schema-clean and says so in its body" and BOTH halves are false: `git rev-list --count a12e651..f327abb` is **30** (25 non-merge), and only **14 of those 25** state their own schema-cleanliness** (the **11** that do not: `f277318`, `18bf524`, `3d5ac44`, `468a96e`, `c408037`, `1407978`, `7e0457b`, `9041b25`, `2efd433`, `1de940e`, `b12b8cb`). **🔴 AND THE 10/15 SPLIT THAT STOOD HERE WAS ITSELF WRONG — a wrong count offered as the correction of a wrong count, which is why this line now carries the method.** Re-derived 2026-08-14 by running `git log -1 --format=%B <sha>` over every one of the 25: the **14 that state it** are `c8b44cf`, `6a28d15`, `f2abdbb`, `82d72cb`, `ef8f24a`, `7540d84`, `4223996`, `4263045`, `50fa892`, `439220d`, `23e0df1`, `6a0268a`, `7fa2d80`, `f327abb` — **four of them (`c8b44cf` "UI-only, no schema change" · `82d72cb` "no schema change, no behaviour change, nothing to migrate" · `7540d84` and `4263045` "Plan only. No code, no schema, no behaviour — `Schema.CurrentVersion` is untouched") had been listed as SILENT.** **⚠️ Two of the 11 silent ones DO contain the word "schema" and are still silent on their own cleanliness:** `3d5ac44` allocates v51/v52/v53 **forward** to WF-1/WF-2/WF-3, and `9041b25` (the census) reports that **the schema version has three answers** — neither says anything about the commit it sits on. **Use `git log -S`, not prose.** `82d72cb` is documentation-only — the class `<summary>` on `Schema` still said `CurrentVersion = 46` while the constant was 50, and it now documents v47/v48/v49/v50; **the constant itself was untouched by that commit.** `plan.md:1274-1275` still allocates v51/v52/v53 forward to WF-1/WF-2/WF-3, unconsumed.
- **▶ THE FOUR "CORE" FIDELITY FIXES — the defects the user hit while actually running the app, all four grounded in the corpus (R7).** In order: 1. **`9608567` — the bill reference is the voucher number, as Tally fills it.** Our F12 knob "Use default Bill-wise details for Bill Allocation" defaulted to **No, with a code comment asserting that matched TallyPrime — the comment was backwards.** Tally ships it Yes; the corpus author turns it off only to demonstrate the sub-screen. Every operator was made to type a reference Tally fills silently, and Receivables stayed empty until they did [TallyPrime *Manage Outstanding Receivables*, "In Name, the sales voucher number appears"; Study Guide p.92]. The allocation is now derived and posted **either way** — the knob governs visibility only. Sales names from the **rendered** voucher number so a configured prefix/suffix is honoured; Purchase prefers Supplier Invoice No. and falls back to the voucher number, and **that fallback is marked in code as an inference, not a documented Tally behaviour.** Fidelity-register row **D3** (`docs/tally-fidelity-defects.md`). 2. **`c8b44cf` — the Ledger master captures an opening balance.** **Without it a set of books could not be opened at all**; an accountant migrating onto this app had no way to state what each ledger carried on day one. UI-only, no schema change (`Ledger.OpeningBalance`/`OpeningIsDebit`/`SignedOpening` already existed and were already consumed by Balance Sheet, Trial Balance, LedgerBook and ImportPlan); field order per Study Guide p.66. **It also fixes an inherited latch defect: confirming the proposed Dr/Cr side never latched it, so a later change of the Under group silently flipped a stated Cr to Dr — a 2× restatement of the opening with no message.** Fidelity-register row **D4**. 3. **`f277318` — Payment, Receipt and Contra open in Single Entry.** All 23 voucher types opened in the Dr/Cr grid because `_mode` was a single unconditional literal, and the doc called it "the default" **citing nothing**; Single Entry existed and worked but was reachable only by Ctrl+H, so the three highest-volume vouchers in the product opened in the wrong layout every time and made the operator take two Dr/Cr side decisions Tally never asks for. **The evidence is inference from absence and the commit says so** — the corpus never tells the reader to switch single entry ON, and three separate passages tell them to switch it OFF. Polarity proven by posting: Receipt/Contra Account = Dr, Payment Account = Cr. Fidelity-register row **D1**. 4. **`a12e651` — negative stock warns instead of blocking (schema v50)**, the single highest-consequence commit in the range: our engine **hard-blocked negative stock across the whole company timeline** while TallyPrime has no block anywhere, only a voucher-screen F12 warning after which the voucher still saves, so **a legitimate sale simply could not be recorded when stock was short.** Fidelity-register rows **D7 / IV-9**.
- **🔴 NEGATIVE STOCK IS NOW POSTABLE, THE VALUATION IS STILL WRONG, AND THERE IS NO WARNING SURFACE — the largest live money risk this range created.** The guard became a non-throwing detector and postings always persist. **I VERIFIED AT HEAD: `InventoryPostingService.NegativeStockWarnings()` (`:184`) has ZERO callers anywhere in `src/` outside its own definition and one `<see cref>` in `Company.cs:257`, and `WarnOnNegativeStock` does not appear in `src/Apex.Desktop` source at all.** So the operator gets **no warning, no toggle and no way to see the shortfall — negative stock posts silently.** `a12e651`'s own body states: *"Valuation is deliberately untouched — how a negative quantity is valued is unresolved and a real-TallyPrime measurement is pending."* **⚠️ `plan.md` slice S-B (`plan.md:940-943`) carries a standing prohibition in terms — *"the ordering is a SAFETY PROPERTY, not a convenience … shipping S-B ahead of a correct valuation would take the measured Balance-Sheet errors from theoretical to LIVE on the `AverageCost` default. Do not reorder."* — and S-B shipped anyway, with valuation untouched. THAT HAS NOW HAPPENED.** plan.md was never amended at the Phase-10.8 section, so **the file is internally contradictory at HEAD in FOUR places, not three** (all re-derived 2026-08-14 against the working-tree `plan.md`): `:15` still says "Phase 10.8 is STOPPED AND BANKED"; `:782-787` — **the fourth site, missed by the first pass and now flagged in the file itself** — said "schema **v49** — negative stock never reached its **v50**"; `:940-943` still says "BLOCKED … Do not reorder"; `:1187` says NS-3/NS-4 shipped. The UI half is queued as **W0-5** (`plan.md:1557`), unbuilt. `CompanyImportRoundTripTests.Failed_apply_…` was **reworked rather than flipped** — it is the only test exercising `ApplyJournal.Rollback` and the guard throwing was its trigger; it now uses a stock-journal imbalance with **odd quantities** so the imbalance cannot be a rounding artefact.
- **🔴 AND THE VALUATION PREMISE ITSELF WAS FALSIFIED IN THIS RANGE — attempt nine must DELETE machinery, not add it.** The kickoff's sourcing pass (`docs/NEXT_SESSION_KICKOFF.md:118-146`) establishes that **TallyPrime's Average Cost has NO repayment model at all**: official formula `Average Cost = Total Cost [Inward Value] / Total Qty [Inward qty] {Annual}`; **sales never touch the pool** ("The Average Cost continues to be Rs.122.50 since there is no change in the Inward Cost"); a Rejection Out / purchase return **does** shrink it. **All eight previous attempts invented a repayment/lot-matching mechanism Tally does not have.** Our `RunAverage` (`StockValuationService.cs:329`) is a *perpetual moving average* whose Outward arm reduces both qty and cost — **on book T3 (buy 10 @ ₹100, sell 5, buy 5 @ ₹200) Tally gives ₹1,333.33 and we give ₹1,500, and T3 NEVER GOES NEGATIVE, so the divergence is not a negative-stock phenomenon: HEAD is wrong on ordinary books.** **⚠️ DO NOT REBUILD THE ORACLE HARNESS — `tools/HeadOracle/` exists, but its AverageCost oracle is `RunAverageDebtAware`, which implements the REFUTED repayment model and will REJECT a correct stateless engine; 30 of 36 AverageCost goldens must be re-derived from the formula and never edited to match code.** Other earned constraints: the conservation/band check is a **tautology** against a stateless pool; never re-rate an existing value; **no floors or clamps** (a positive-qty floor was tried and *hid* a real error, turning a diagnosable −₹120 into a plausible ₹0); Average Cost only; do not change the godown dimension in the same slice.
- **▶ STREAM A — "WRONG FIGURES" (Phase 10.10; `c408037`, `1407978`, `7e0457b`, merged clean at `c4caeed`).** (1) **`c408037` (WF-4 + WF-5) — interest was LOST, not deferred, for every bill raised inside the reporting window.** The "Always" basis accrued only on the balance that already existed at the **start** of the window, so a bill raised inside the period produced **no row at all**, and those days were never recovered by a later report; any book whose activity happened during the window it was asked about under-reported interest **silently, with nothing on the report to say a row was missing.** Replaced with a running-balance accrual from the day after each transaction ["the interest is calculated from the date of the transaction", CORPUS-BOOK p.118, extracted line 4264]. Day-count basis is now resolved **per accrual segment from the segment's own start date** (`InterestCalculation.BasisFor` at `src/Apex.Ledger/Reports/InterestCalculation.cs:473`), so a window spanning a month/year boundary no longer prices its later days against the earlier period's divisor, and each `InterestPer` member now documents the divisor the engine **actually uses** rather than the one it was intended to use — "that disagreement is precisely how IV-8 survived unnoticed". The Principal column is now the **time-weighted average balance over the row's segment**, so `Principal × Rate × Days / Basis == Interest` holds on the printed row and an auditor can re-derive the figure from the four printed columns; previously that identity failed whenever the balance moved mid-segment. (2) **`1407978` (WF-8) — an order was never retired, and the ROOT CAUSE WAS IN THE SHELL.** `InventoryRegisters.BuildOrders` hard-coded `FulfilledQuantity: 0m, OutstandingQuantity: line.Quantity`, so **the Order Register reported every order fully outstanding for ever.** New engine `src/Apex.Ledger/Reports/OrderFulfilment.cs` (public entry `OutstandingByItem` at `:351`), built over the **whole book up to `to`** rather than over `[from,to]` — had a pre-window order been absent from the cohort its movement would have been **misattributed to a newer in-window order**, i.e. a report whose window silently changed the figures it reported; only the *listing* is windowed. **🔴 THE FIRST IMPLEMENTATION WAS AN ENGINE-ONLY FIX AND WAS A NO-OP ON EVERY REAL BOOK**: the party picker was gated `IsVisible="{Binding IsOrder}"`, hidden on every Receipt/Delivery/Rejection-In/Rejection-Out note, and `BuildMovementNote` never passed `partyId`, so **every note this product can actually key posted a null party and a cohort derived on (PartyId, StockItemId) could never match one — and its 18 green tests all built a voucher shape the UI cannot produce, which is why they passed while the product stayed broken.** Both halves fixed here. Party per note type is corpus-grounded [Receipt Note supplier p.71 · Delivery Note customer p.77 · Rejection In "customer who rejected item" p.51 · Rejection Out supplier p.53]; naming a party posts **no accounting entry** ["will not reflect in Ledger/party balance … will only affect your stock", pp.70, 76] = this product's DP-5 rule; Stock Journal and Physical Stock deliberately do **not** show it. **FOUR KNOWN-WRONG RESIDUALS SHIPPED DELIBERATELY, three of which UNDER-state shortfall (the dangerous direction):** R1 a duplicated inward document under-states shortfall (a genuine stock double-count, not an attribution error — a tracking number would not fix it); R2 a duplicate document's surplus can fully retire a **second, untouched** order; R3 a movement against a **cancelled** order retires the live order behind it; R4 Rejection In is not netted back out of fulfilment. Several fixtures are deliberate **change-detectors: they assert the wrong figure with the correct one written beside it.** It also **reverts a false claim made in passing** — `QuantityInBase` had been widened to `internal` with a doc saying `OrderFulfilment` normalised through it "so the two cannot drift"; `OrderFulfilment` contains **no reference to it** and reads `InventoryMovements.Between`, which owns its own base-unit conversion (`InventoryMovements.cs:161`, `:186`), so the widening was dead and the comment pointed a maintainer at the wrong code path. **The OrderFulfilment fixtures ship in the FOLLOWING commit on purpose**, because three assert against `ReorderStatusRow.NettAvailable` which `7e0457b` introduces — landing them here would leave `Apex.Ledger.Tests` uncompilable at this revision and break `git bisect` across the slice. (3) **`7e0457b` (WF-7 / IV-10) — reorder status ignored stock already committed on open sales orders.** Shortfall was measured against **closing stock alone** and Sales Orders Due were **fetched, assigned and never read**, so an item whose closing quantity sat above its reorder level but whose stock was already committed was **never listed, nothing was ordered and the goods went undelivered — the report was least useful exactly when it mattered, on a busy item with a full order book.** **Nett Available = `closing + pendingPO − soDue`** (`src/Apex.Ledger/Reports/ReorderStatus.cs:25`, appended LAST because the record is positional); both order legs read `OrderFulfilment.OutstandingByItem`, **so the Order Register and Reorder Status draw both WHICH orders count and HOW MUCH of each from the same map and cannot drift on the cancelled/post-dated predicate or on quantity**, and `ReorderStatus.PendingOrderQty`'s parallel ordered-quantity sum was deleted rather than left beside it. **The pre-10.10 listing predicate — flag exactly the items at or below level — was INVENTED and appears in no Tally source**; TallyHelp's Reorder Status page states that by default all stock items in the selected group/category display, with **F8 (Reorder Only)** as the user's filter, so every item that resolves a reorder level is now listed and the single surviving engine-side exclusion is "no reorder level resolved". **A test whose NAME asserted the OPPOSITE of its own body** — `Reorder_status_at_exactly_the_level_is_flagged`, whose assertions say the item is *not* flagged, which is correct under the corpus's strict "more than" rule — survived the WF-7 rewrite and was renamed.
- **▶ STREAM B — VOUCHER LIFECYCLE (Phase 10.11; `6a28d15`, `f2abdbb`, merged clean at `08751e0`).** (1) **`6a28d15` (VL-2 step 1) — quick jumps and the accept prompt stopped answering modifier chords.** `CanQuickJump` tested only `IsMenuScreen && !IsTyping(e)` and the `switch (e.Key)` consulting it tested no modifiers either, so **all four report quick-jumps fired for every chord no earlier arm had claimed: Alt+D opened the Day Book and so did Ctrl+D and Shift+D; Alt+B/Alt+P/Alt+T the same.** **Sequenced deliberately AHEAD of the delete slice**, because that slice binds Alt+D to DELETE and binding a destructive verb on top of a chord that already fires a navigation would make a stray Alt+D both destructive and ambiguous, with the winner depending on the screen. The guard is `e.KeyModifiers == KeyModifiers.None` placed **in the predicate, not on the four arms** (a per-arm fix on D would have left three survivors), identical to the WI-2/WI-9 bare-letter menu arm, and it excludes **Shift** deliberately, not merely Alt. **A SECOND HOLE, AND THE FIRST FIX FOR IT WAS ITSELF A DEFECT CAUGHT IN REVIEW:** the WI-11 accept prompt excluded Control but not Alt, so a stray **Alt+Y** — the Data → Backup/Restore accelerator, live on every screen a company is open on — reached `ConfirmMasterAccept` and **SAVED the master**; the first cut narrowed it with `case Key.Y when !altHeld`, which **YIELDED** Alt+Y to `ShowDataMenu` → `SelectRootItem` → `TrimColumnsAfter(0)` → `ClearSubScreens`, so the ledger was not created **but the typed name, the chosen group and the opening balance were gone with no message** — and under Alt+C create-on-the-fly `TrimColumnsAfter(0)` takes the **VOUCHER** column too, so **a half-keyed invoice dies to a menu chord.** *"The fix had stopped an unconfirmed save and bought an unconfirmed DESTROY in its place."* Final behaviour: **Alt+Y and Alt+N are CONSUMED AND INERT while a confirmation is up** — the prompt is modal against Alt+letter chords, they change nothing and the question stays on screen; two presses, the doctrine already settled for Escape. **Escape is deliberately EXEMPT** (not a letter, owns no Alt accelerator, and the arm it would fall through to is `case Key.Escape when !IsPickerOpen(e)` → `Back()`, which pops the Miller column and discards the half-typed master — the same work-loss class), so Alt+Escape still ANSWERS the prompt and a test pins it. `QuickJumpModifierGuardTests` pins **both directions** — bare D/B/P/T still open their reports and bare Y/N still answer the prompt — so the narrowing cannot be "fixed" by reopening the hole. (2) **`f2abdbb` (VL-4 / IV-5) — settlement came off Ctrl+B, and Alt+A now OPENS a voucher instead of posting one.** **Ctrl+B was bound app-wide and unconditionally — it handled and returned regardless of screen — and called `SettleBills()`, which POSTED a real Receipt or Payment for every spacebar-selected bill: always the bill's FULL pending amount, always through a ledger literally named "Cash", dated at the report's as-of, with no preview, no confirmation and no undo.** In TallyPrime **Ctrl+B is "Basis of Values"**, a report option that re-bases how figures are computed and presented and **writes nothing to the books** [TallyHelp keyboard-shortcuts, Reports]; TallyPrime's Bills Outstanding has no settlement action of any kind, a bill being settled by keying a Receipt/Payment and choosing Against Reference from the List of Pending Bills [CORPUS-SG p.92 §5.5]. **So an operator pressing Ctrl+B on Bills Receivable to change how figures DISPLAY instead posted a batch of receipt vouchers against their debtors — a trap armed by a correct Tally reflex (Spacebar = select line in report) and sprung by a second.** `SettleBills`/`SettleSelected`/`SettleAndPost` are deleted with zero surviving call sites, and the Ctrl+B button-bar row was deleted too — **leaving it would paint an accelerator badge for a key that fires nothing (register defect IV-31).** **`BuildSettlementAllocations` deliberately SURVIVES**: it is the only code in the repo that validates an AgstRef against a genuinely open bill and caps each knock at that bill's pending amount — the in-voucher Bill-wise panel does not (fidelity-register defect **D5**) — so deleting it alongside the posting would have made settlement **less** safe than the defect being replaced; its cap moved **from per-knock to AGGREGATE per reference**, because operator-editable rows let two rows name the SAME bill, each under the pending yet summing well over it, after which the bill vanishes from the report and the **"Σ open bills == ledger closing balance" invariant breaks silently.** Replacement: **Alt+A on Outstandings** opens a Single Entry Receipt/Payment **pre-loaded** with the selected bills as Against-Reference allocations, the operator confirming date, cash/bank ledger and every per-bill amount — Alt+A is TallyPrime's own "Add voucher in report" [TallyHelp Reports bottom bar; CORPUS-BOOK p.431] and is already this app's meaning for Alt+A on the Day Book. **⚠️ CTRL+B IS NOW FREE AND RESERVED AND "BASIS OF VALUES" IS NOT BUILT — recorded as named debt, not an oversight. It must reach nothing rather than be squatted by an unrelated feature, and with no arm there it falls through toward the bare-letter quick-jump switch, which is harmless ONLY because `6a28d15` narrowed `CanQuickJump` to `e.KeyModifiers == KeyModifiers.None`. That guard must not be loosened; a test pins it.** **PROVENANCE, recorded honestly in the commit: this slice's engineer died on a session limit after writing to disk and never reported**, so the work as found carried no red-proof, no restoration proof and no review; it was then put through three adversarial lenses and a fix pass, which **found and fixed real defects rather than confirming the work.** The stale `HeadlessMainWindowTests` fixture — whose own name asserted "CtrlB settles it" — was **converted to the Alt+A route rather than deleted**, keeping its render assertions verbatim, and its amount was moved to **odd paisa (25,417.83)** so the rendered-text assertion also catches a paisa slip; the two `VoucherTypeNavigationIdentityTests` cases were likewise rewritten onto Alt+A and never dropped, the rule under test ("a settlement is an ordinary Receipt the operator could have keyed by hand, so if they could not have keyed it we must not produce it for them") asserted verbatim with only the key that reaches it moved.

(`docs/NEXT_SESSION_KICKOFF.md:93-98`), not fidelity-register or gap-decision ones — see the collision warning at the top of this entry.** **Diverged-rule D1 pro-rata apportionment** → new `src/Apex.Ledger/ProRata.cs` (`Rupees` at `:44`, `Paisa` at `:52`), three copies collapsed (`Gstr1.cs:670`, `EInvoiceJson.cs:528`, `EWayBillJson.cs:223` are now one-line delegations) — **🔴 but the premise this row started from was WRONG and saying so is the point: the missing zero-guard was reported as a live divide-by-zero in a filed return and it is NOT REACHABLE**, because both GSTR-1 call sites already `continue` on `groupValue == 0m` (`Gstr1.cs:629`, `:808`) before entering the loop and those six calls (`:646-648`, `:824-826`) are the only way in. **That makes the caller-side guards LOAD-BEARING, not redundant leftovers: they SKIP the group, and deleting one on the belief the shared `== 0` covers it would NOT produce zeros — it would send every non-final leg to 0 while the remainder branch dumped the group's entire posted tax onto its last leg, a silently wrong filed return.** The guard stays `== 0` and **not `<= 0`**, because on a credit note the leg value and the group value carry the same sign, so widening it would zero the tax split on **every negative-value document**. **Diverged-rule D2 Indian digit grouping** → new `src/Apex.Ledger/IndianMoneyFormat.cs`: **the same money printed two different ways from the same assembly** — ₹1,00,000 rendered `1,00,000.00` on a Form-16A/27D certificate and `100,000.00` on the tax invoice, the POS receipt and the printed voucher (all `"#,##0.00"` against `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`, group size a flat 3 = **Western**), **and `CertificatePdfSupport`'s own doc comment claimed it mirrored `InvoicePdf`; it did not.** A third form was worse: an interpolated `$"₹{x.Amount:#,##0}"` binds to the **HOST's** culture, so the TDS/TCS nature-of-payment masters printed a §194C **₹1,00,000** threshold as **"₹100,000"** on an en-US host and **"₹100.000"** on a de-DE one, **where it reads as a decimal** — and those sites are lakh-scale by law, i.e. always above the grouping boundary. **R7 sourcing:** Tally exposes digit style as a **CURRENCY-MASTER property**, the single field "Show Amounts in Millions" on the Company-creation and Currency-creation screens [`664311548-Tally-Prime-Book.pdf`], so Millions is an explicit opt-in, the default **is** lakh/crore grouping, and because the setting lives on the currency it applies uniformly. The shared culture (`Culture` at `:61`) is `CultureInfo.ReadOnly`-frozen (`:70`), **because consolidating nine call sites onto one object makes it a process-wide global and an earlier-running test could otherwise set `NumberGroupSizes = new[]{3}` and silently revert every invoice, receipt, voucher and grid to Western grouping as order-dependent cross-test contamination.** **Diverged-rule D3 rupees → integer paisa** → new `src/Apex.Ledger/PaisaConversion.cs`: **EIGHT implementations carrying TWO INCOMPATIBLE SEMANTICS with nothing in their names to tell them apart** — a sub-paisa amount **THREW** at the persist/export boundary and was **SILENTLY ROUNDED** on six report/service paths. Both semantics are kept because both are correct in their own place **but now NAMED** — `ToPaisaExact` (`:44`, persist/export) and `ToPaisaRounded` (`:62`, derived report/set-off arithmetic), deliberately not collapsed (`:17-35`) — since collapsing them would either crash GSTR-2B reconciliation and ITC set-off **or corrupt stored money**; **`GstConfig.ReconTolerance` (`Domain/GstConfig.cs:135`) turned out to be a THIRD semantics**, a bare `(long)` cast, i.e. truncation toward zero, which **NARROWED a sub-paisa tolerance by a paisa instead of rounding it.** **Verified in `src/`: 12 `PaisaConversion.ToPaisaRounded` call sites (18 textual hits, 6 of them `<see cref>`), 2 `ToPaisaExact`, 2 `TryToPaisaExact`, 2 `IsPaisaExact`.** **Diverged-rule D7 HSN/SAC resolution ORDER** → `GstReportSupport.HsnSacOf` (`GstReportSupport.cs:106`), hand-written at four call sites so the order was four copies free to drift — **🔴 but the SENTINEL is deliberately NOT unified and that half matters more**: the shared rule stops at resolution and returns `null`, and each consumer spells absence its own way because these are **four correct answers to four different questions** — the human-read GSTR-1 summary labels the bucket `(none)` (a blank row key would read as a rendering fault); INV-01 and EWB-01 file `HsnCd: ""` because the NIC schema types the field as a string and the empty string is the department's own "not declared", **so filing the literal text `(none)` into a statutory code field would be a malformed submission**; the Rule-46 invoice leaves the column blank because a statutory document omits a field it has no value for. **Unifying the sentinel would have broken three of the four.** **🔴 THE MONEY DEFECT NOBODY HAD FLAGGED: 16 TRUNCATING CASTS ON MONEY PERSISTENCE in `src/Apex.Persistence.Sqlite/SqliteCompanyStore.cs`**, every one the bare `(long)(x.Amount * 100m)` — **truncation toward zero: not rounding, and not the refusal the store's own codec performs** — writing **the Gratuity cap (`:4621`), the Bonus calculation ceiling and minimum wage (`:4628`, `:4629`), all nine Chapter-VI-A / previous-employer fields of an employee's tax declaration (`:4700-4708`), and the Professional-Tax band from/to/amount (`:4734-4736`) plus its month override (`:2849`)**, so **a sub-paisa value reaching any of them lost its tail silently and permanently, in the system of record.** All 16 now route through `Paisa.FromDecimal` (the EXACT codec, which refuses instead of truncating) — **I verified: `Paisa.FromDecimal` occurs exactly 16 times IN THAT FILE (19 across `src/` as a whole, the three extras being doc comments at `PaisaConversion.cs:12` and `ForexGainLoss.cs:150,:153`), and the `(long)(… * 100m)` form occurs ZERO times in `src/` as a whole.** Related on the way IN: `TaxDeclarationViewModel.TryMoney` had **no sub-paisa guard at all** (now `:251-256`), and the consequence was worse than a bad error message — **`Save` mutates the SHARED in-memory Company via `AddTaxDeclaration` BEFORE the store is asked to persist and neither validates, so a sub-paisa figure entered the aggregate, the save threw, the catch reported it, and the bad declaration REMAINED in the Company, making every later, unrelated save throw from a call site that has no try/catch.** **TWO ROWS WERE REFUSED AND BOTH REFUSALS ARE THE POINT: diverged-rule D8 is a FALSE POSITIVE** — the claim that `"0.##"` renders a 0.125% rate as `0.13%` **cannot be true, because every `RateBasisPoints` in the domain is an `int`, so `bp / 100m` has at most TWO decimal places and 0.125% is not representable in basis points at all** (proved exhaustively rather than argued); **a shared "rate formatter" would have been a home for a rule that does not vary — churn justified by a defect that is not real.** **Diverged-rule D6 was deliberately NOT unified**: the two `ApplyRounding` methods **do** disagree on negatives — **interest rounding is magnitude-based (`Domain/InterestParameters.cs:91-109`; Upward on −100.4 gives −101, away from zero) while payroll rounding is signed (`Services/PayrollComputationService.cs:747-771`; Ceiling(−100.4) gives −100, toward +∞)** — but different enums, different parameterisation and different statutory domains mean **forcing them onto one method would have silently changed one domain's arithmetic to suit the other's, precisely the FALSE UNIFICATION this slice was warned against.** **THE DURABLE HALF is `tests/Apex.Ledger.Tests/OneRuleDriftLockTests.cs`** (322 lines, 10 `[Fact]`s), which scans the shipped `src/` tree and fails if any unified rule sprouts a second copy — **eight single-pattern locks plus one file-level co-occurrence lock** for the multi-line `var scaled = x * 100m; … decimal.Truncate(scaled)` idiom that no single-line regex can see and that renaming one local defeats — **and the locks assert their own NON-VACUITY rather than assuming it**, running the SAME named pattern constants over faithful reconstructions of the removed copies including variable-renamed and line-split variants, **so "the scan found nothing" cannot silently mean "the pattern matches nothing"**; a separate test fails if the scan stops reading the shipped tree, and another asserts the file-level paisa lock does **not** fire on the tree's other integer scales (interest millis ×1,000, forex/quantity micros ×1,000,000), **because a lock that false-positives is a lock a future maintainer deletes.** **⚠️ Honest limits recorded in the file itself (`:26-28`): these match the textual idiom, so a copy that restructures the expression entirely can still slip past — a ratchet, not a proof of uniqueness. Home files are also exempted by BARE FILENAME (`:117`, `:147`), so a new file named e.g. `ProRata.cs` anywhere under `src/` is silently exempt, and the scan does not cover `tests/`.** **🔴 A NEW TEST THAT CANNOT FAIL SHIPPED WITH IT:** `UnifiedRuleBehaviourTests.BasisPointFormatsAreIdenticalForEveryRepresentableRate` (`:274-283`) touches **no product code at all** — it loops 200,001 integers formatting `bp / 100m` two ways and comparing them, and **would stay green if every basis-point rendering in the app were rewritten.** It is a decision record wearing a `[Fact]`. **And the commit's stated reason for skipping the two untouched rows is FALSE:** it says `IsInterState` "was already single-homed by W0-8 (`4223996`) — which is why that file already carries 'The ONE copy of this rule'"; **that sentence sits on `IsOverseasStateCode` (`GstReportSupport.cs:110`, predicate at `:131`), a different rule, and `git log --all -S "private bool IsInterState(Voucher voucher)"` returns only `c915318` (Phase 9 S5) — it predates W0-8, which did not remove it.** **STILL OPEN AFTER THE SLICE (3 of 8 closed):** diverged-rule (d) `IsInterState` — **two live implementations disagreeing on the null-home case**, `GstService.cs:332-339` **throws** where `EWayBillService.cs:145-150` **returns false**, with six `src/` callers on the throwing form; and (e) place of supply — **three derivations** (`GstReportSupport.cs:74-79` party-then-home · `VoucherPrintProjector.cs:755-760` home-fallback only when intra-state, **blank** on inter-state · `EInvoiceJson.cs:267` `"96"`-then-party-then-home), so **a B2C inter-state supply with no recorded party state gets home code from one and blank from another.** **No drift lock covers either. Deciding them is STATUTORY, not de-duplication — sequence with GST routing work and do NOT unify by picking whichever copy is convenient.** Residuals: (h) is two-thirds discharged — **10 host-culture-bound `$"{x:0.##}"` rate renderings survive** (`Cmp08ReportViewModel.cs:173`, `GstConfigViewModel.cs:413`, `InterestReportViewModel.cs:74`, `LedgerMasterViewModel.cs:1169`, `NatureOfGoodsMasterViewModel.cs:134,179,180`, `NatureOfPaymentMasterViewModel.cs:144,191,192`), and `GstConfigViewModel.cs:413`'s `{bp / 200m:0.##}` half-rate genuinely CAN carry a third decimal (125 bp ⇒ 0.625% ⇒ prints "0.63") which the exhaustive proof never covers; (b) leaves **one money site still Western-grouped and outside every lock**, `ForexReportViewModel.cs:260` `"#,##0.##"`/Invariant, used at `:124` for `ForexBalance` — **whether a FOREIGN-currency balance should be Indian-grouped at all is a design question the slice never asks.** **D6 is pinned on the INTEREST side only (`UnifiedRuleBehaviourTests.cs:298-313`); grepped `tests/` — ZERO references to `PayHeadRoundingMethod.Upward`/`.Downward`, so converting the payroll side to magnitude semantics would still pass green.** **🔴 AND THE SLICE CONVERTED A SILENT-TRUNCATION DEFECT INTO A PERSISTENT ONE ON TWO SCREENS — its highest-value open item.** `Paisa.FromDecimal` now **throws** where it truncated, and the tax-declaration screen got the paired front-line guard, but the **Gratuity cap** and the **Bonus ceiling / minimum wage** did not: `GstConfigViewModel` is their only writer, parses with `TryParseWholeRupees` (`:1254-1256`) which **accepts a decimal point despite the name** (a plain `decimal.TryParse`, only further check `< 0m`), and `ApplyGratuity` → `PayrollService.EnableGratuity` (`src/Apex.Ledger/Services/PayrollService.cs:218-230`) sets the config **on the shared Company BEFORE `_storage.Save`** while the catch reverts only the toggle — **so a sub-paisa cap poisons the in-memory aggregate and every LATER save throws.** The other 13 casts are safe (the nine declaration fields are guarded; the four PT values have **no UI writer at all** — no `new ProfessionalTaxBand` in `src/`, the only inbound path being canonical XML through `MoneyCodec.FromPaisa(long)`, exact by construction). **Fix it with the same `IsPaisaExact` front-line guard already written for `TaxDeclarationViewModel.TryMoney`, before any other row.**

- **▶ R12 USER DECISIONS — recorded WITH DATES because nothing else in the repo evidences them.** 1. **(2026-08-10) The 9 obsolete-by-law pre-GST capabilities (VAT, CST, Service Tax, Excise) WILL NOT BE BUILT — with TDS/TCS explicitly carved out: "clone the mechanism, never the numbers"** (`plan.md:367` ff., written by `2efd433`). 2. **(2026-08-10) A composition dealer's voucher carrying posted forward tax is REFUSED AT ACCEPT** rather than printed as an unclassifiable document; `VoucherValidator` rejects the shape on every entry path, citing §10(4) by name and naming the fix. **"Carries forward tax" is answered off the GENERAL LEDGER, not off line metadata, and that is load-bearing: only the GST-engine accept paths stamp `GstLineTax`, and the shipped Sales AS-VOUCHER screen stamps none — it builds every leg as a plain `new EntryLine(...)` over an unfiltered ledger picker — so a composition dealer's hand-keyed `Cr Output CGST / Cr Output SGST` was invisible to every metadata-only predicate and the guard would have accepted the very entry it exists to refuse.** **⚠️ EXISTING BOOKS STILL OPEN: the guard is scoped to the ENTRY path and does not run on rehydration**, because `SqliteCompanyStore.Load` re-posts every stored voucher through this engine and an unconditional rule would make a book that ALREADY contains the shape **unopenable** — which is also **why the print-path refusal is not dead code and must not be deleted.** **KNOWN NAMING DEBT, DEFERRED:** the guard is gated on `CostAllocationStrictness == Strict` because that enum is **already** the entry-vs-rehydration discriminator; it works and is now misnamed for what it gates, and the rename touches **~13 files across four test projects**, so it is left to its own slice (recorded in the doc comments of `VoucherValidator.EnsureValid` and `LedgerService.Post`). 3. **(2026-08-10) A wholly-exempt service (Accounting Invoice) sale is a BILL OF SUPPLY, not a tax invoice** [CGST §31(3)(c) binds "a registered person supplying exempted goods OR SERVICES or both"; §2(47)]. **Recorded as a DECISION rather than as behaviour because of how it got here: a previous pass FLIPPED this on its own authority, reversing an earlier user decision, and the pre-existing test written to pin the old behaviour STAYED GREEN through the inversion because it asserted a different predicate. The user has now explicitly ratified the flip.** 4. **(2026-08-14) A composition dealer's outward movement carrying posted forward tax files `BIL`, not `INV`.** Unifying the print and filing predicates would have flipped that shape's `docType` **accidentally**, because the unified predicate's first gate is `CarriesForwardTax` and **that gate exists for a PRINT-MONEY reason** (a bill of supply shows no tax, so titling a tax-bearing document one would print a Grand Total short of the posted party leg); **the NIC `docType` field carries no money at all — the money gate was silently governing a FILING field it has no business reaching**, and §31(3)(c) is unconditional for a §10 person. Implemented as `GstReportSupport.IsBillOfSupplyForFiling` (`:793`), read by `EWayBillService.PartACodesFor` and **by nothing on the print path**. **THE RULING WAS DELIBERATELY NOT EXTENDED TO THE EXEMPT LIMB AND THE ASYMMETRY IS THE WHOLE POINT: §10(4) bars a composition dealer from collecting tax absolutely, so posted tax on his voucher is an anomaly that says nothing about the supply — whereas nothing bars a REGULAR dealer from collecting tax, so HIS posted tax is positive evidence the supply was not exempt and the money gate is reading real signal there. Same gate, opposite evidentiary value; extending the ruling would have destroyed that.** 5. **(2026-08-10, recorded on `7e0457b`) HARD GATE PR-8 RETIRED** [CORPUS-BOOK p.164] — the minimum order quantity no longer floors the order at zero shortfall, so an adequately covered item reads Order To Be Placed 0 instead of being told to buy a full MOQ it does not need; the MOQ floor still applies when there **is** a real shortfall. **This requires amending `docs/phase6-advanced-inventory-requirements.md` and that has not been done.** 6. **STILL OPEN AND REQUIRED, not taken:** whether `EInvoiceService.DocumentNumberOf` should keep emitting the document number as typed. **Four ordinary numbering configurations violate NIC's `^([A-Z1-9]{1}[A-Z0-9/-]{0,15})$` pattern** — zero-padded width, space-padded width, lowercase prefix, and `NumberingMethod.None` (a 32-char lowercase GUID, over `maxLength: 16`) — but emitting as-typed is the whole of the one-string contract (paper == IRP == e-Way == GSTR-1 == B2C-QR == Day Book), **so behaviour is deliberately unchanged and pinned by `PINNED_the_document_number_is_not_guarded_against_the_schema_pattern` rather than laundered.** **▶ AND TWO EARLIER R12 DECISIONS FROM THE PRECEDING ENTRY ARE STILL OPEN AND WERE NOT TOUCHED IN THIS RANGE — carried forward here so the latest entry's backlog is complete:** **(2026-08-02) goods-return STOCK PARITY on Credit/Debit Note (gap-decision `D3`, `docs/tally-gap-decisions.md`) is APPROVED BEHIND AN ORACLE AND STILL NOT STARTED** — **verified unchanged at `src/Apex.Ledger/Services/ItemInvoiceStock.cs:53`**, which still reads `return type is not null && type.BaseType is VoucherBaseType.Purchase or VoucherBaseType.Sales;`, so a Credit Note carries no inventory lines at all: a sales return credits the customer but leaves the goods off the books, a purchase return debits the supplier but leaves phantom goods on hand, and **the drift COMPOUNDS with every return**; Robert & Bright must stay byte-identical. **And the remaining gap decisions D2, D6, D8, D10, D11, D14–D24 are STILL UNANSWERED — `docs/tally-gap-decisions.md` was not touched anywhere in this range** (`git diff --stat 6124a25..f327abb -- docs/` lists exactly five files and it is not one of them) — **nothing is built from them without an R6 `plan.md` amendment first.**
